), and that the parameters
\alpha_{i}
,
\beta_{i}
and
\gamma_{i}
satisfy (
2.60
).

We begin this section by adjusting the geometrical parameters to ensure some important orthogonality relations for the difference
\epsilon
between the solution
v
and the approximate solution
V
with respect to some natural directions. We then state and prove some estimates for the dynamical systems satisfied by the geometric parameters and for the
H^{1}
norm of the difference
\epsilon
.

3.1.
Modulation close to the approximate solution

For any
Z^{0}>1
,
\mu^{0}>0
and
\eta>0
, we define the set of parameters
\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}
and the tube
\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\eta}
around the approximate solution
V(\cdot;\Gamma)
by

\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Gamma=(z_{1},z_{2},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{6}\,:\,|z_{1}-z_{2}|>Z^{0};\\
&|\mu_{1}|<\mu^{0};\ |\mu_{2}|<\mu^{0};\ |\omega_{1}|<\mu^{0};\ |\omega_{2}|<\mu^{0}\end{aligned}\right\},

\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\sigma}:=\left\{w\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\,:\,\inf_{\Gamma\in\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}}\left\|w-V(\Gamma)\right\|_{H^{1}}<\sigma\right\}.

Lemma 3.1
.

There exists
C>0
,
\sigma^{\star}>0
,
Z_{1}^{\star}>Z_{0}^{\star}
and
\nu_{1}^{\star}\in(0,\nu_{0}^{\star})
such that the following holds. For any
\sigma<\sigma^{\star}
,
Z^{0}>Z_{1}^{\star}
and
\mu^{0}<\nu_{1}^{\star}
, let
w\in C(I:H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
be a solution to (
1.10
) such that
w(t)\in\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\sigma}
for all
t\in I
. Then there exists a unique
\mathcal{C}^{1}
function
\Gamma(t)=\left(z_{1}(t),z_{2}(t),\omega_{1}(t),\omega_{2}(t),\mu_{1}(t),\mu_{2}(t)\right)
from
I
to
\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}
such that by defining

\epsilon(t):=w(t)-V(\Gamma(t)),

(3.1)

we have for all
t\in I
and for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\int\epsilon(t)\partial_{x}R_{i}(t)=\int\epsilon(t)\partial_{y}R_{i}(t)=\int\epsilon(t)R_{i}(t)=0,

(3.2)

\displaystyle|z_{1}(t)-z_{2}(t)|>Z^{0}-C\sigma,\quad|\omega_{i}(t)|<\mu^{0}+C\sigma,\quad|\mu_{i}(t)|<\mu^{0}+C\sigma,

(3.3)

\displaystyle\left\|\epsilon(t)\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\sigma.

(3.4)

Moreover,
\epsilon
satisfies on
I

\partial_{t}\epsilon+\partial_{x}(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+(V+\epsilon)^{2}-V^{2})=-\mathcal{E}_{V},

(3.5)

where
\mathcal{E}_{V}
is defined in (
2.26
).

Remark 3.2
.

If we assume moreover that for some
\Gamma^{0}=(z_{1}^{0},z_{2}^{0},\omega_{1}^{0},\omega_{2}^{0},\mu_{1}^{0},\mu_{2}^{0})\in\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}
and for some
t_{0}\in I
, we have
\|w(t_{0})-V(\Gamma^{0})\|_{H^{1}}<\sigma
, then in addition to (
3.2
)-(
3.4
), we have for
i=1,2
, that

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|z_{i}(t_{0})-z_{i}^{0}|+|\mu_{i}(t_{0})-\mu_{i}^{0}|+|\omega_{i}(t_{0})-\omega_{i}^{0}|\right)\leq C\sigma.

(3.6)

Proof.

The proof of the existence, uniqueness and continuity of
\Gamma
relies on classical arguments based on a qualitative version of the implicit function theorem for functions independent of time (see for example Lemma 3.1 in
[
41
]
or Proposition 2.2 in
[
14
]
and the references therein).

More precisely, if we define

\Phi:\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\sigma}\times\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}\to\mathbb{R}^{6},\ (w,\Gamma)\mapsto\left(\int\epsilon\partial_{x}R_{1},\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{1},\int\epsilon R_{1},\int\epsilon\partial_{x}R_{2},\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{2},\int\epsilon R_{2}\right),

the main point consists in verifying that
d_{\Gamma}\Phi(w,\Gamma)
is invertible. This is ensured by decomposing
d_{\Gamma}\Phi(w,\Gamma)=D+A
, where

\displaystyle D=\text{diag}\left(-\int(\partial_{x}Q)^{2},-\int(\partial_{y}Q)^{2},\int Q\Lambda Q,-\int(\partial_{x}Q)^{2},-\int(\partial_{y}Q)^{2},\int Q\Lambda Q\right)

and by using (
B.2
) and
\|A\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq e^{-\frac{1}{2}Z^{0}}+\mu^{0}
thanks to (
2.35
), Lemma
B.4
and Lemma
C.1
. Thus, it follows by taking
Z^{0}
large enough, and
\mu^{0}
and
\sigma
small enough that there exist a unique
C^{1}
function
\Gamma:\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\sigma}\to\Xi_{Z^{0},\mu^{0}}
such that
\Phi(w,\Gamma(w))=0
, for any
w\in\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\sigma}
.

Now let
w\in C(I:H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
be a solution to (
1.10
) such that
w(t)\in\mathcal{U}_{Z^{0},\mu^{0},\sigma}
for all
t\in I
. By abuse of notation, we define
\Gamma(t):=\Gamma(v(t))
, so that
\Gamma\in C^{0}(I)
. The fact that
\Gamma
is of class
C^{1}
follows then from the Cauchy-Lipschitz theorem (see for example Proposition 2.2 in
[
14
]
). Moreover, (
3.5
) is deduced from (
1.10
) and (
2.26
).

Finally, Remark
3.2
follows directly from the uniqueness of the function
\Gamma
in the neighborhood of
V(\Gamma^{0})
.
∎

3.2.
Control of the time derivative of the geometric parameters

We derive estimates for the differential system satisfied by the geometric parameters
\Gamma=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},z_{1},z_{2},\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
.

Proposition 3.3
.

We have, for all
t\in I
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left|-\dot{z}_{i}+\mu_{i}+\alpha_{i}\right|+\left|\dot{\omega}_{i}-\beta_{i}\right|\right)\lesssim\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int S\partial_{x}R_{i}\right|+\|T\|_{L^{2}}+\left(e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.8)

Remark 3.4
.

Estimates (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) still hold if one replace
\|T\|_{L^{2}}
by
\int|T|\left(|R_{1}|+|R_{2}|\right)
and
\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}
by
\int|\partial_{x}S|\left(|R_{1}|+|R_{2}|\right)
on the right-hand side.

Remark 3.5
.

We deduce from Proposition
3.3
some rough bounds on the time derivative of the geometrical parameters
z_{i}
,
\omega_{i}
,
\gamma_{i}
. More precisely, it holds for all
t\in I
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left|\dot{z}_{i}\right|+\left|\dot{\omega}_{i}\right|\right)\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|+\frac{e^{-z}}{\sqrt{z}}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\dot{\mu}_{i}\right|\lesssim\frac{e^{-z}}{\sqrt{z}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int S\partial_{x}R_{i}\right|+\|T\|_{L^{2}}+\left(e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.10)

Proof.

We first claim that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|-\dot{z}_{i}+\mu_{i}+\alpha_{i}\right|+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|-\dot{\omega}_{i}+\beta_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\right|+\left(1+\sum_{i=1}\left(|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{z}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)\right)\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}};

(3.11)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right|\lesssim e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\right|+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int S\partial_{x}R_{i}\right|+\|T\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.12)

To prove (
3.11
), we compute the time derivative of the orthogonality relations in (
3.2
). Let us detail the computations for the first orthogonality relation with
i=1
. By using (
3.5
) and (
2.42
), we infer that

\displaystyle 0=\int\epsilon\frac{d}{dt}\partial_{x}R_{1}-\int\partial_{x}\left(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+2V\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}R_{1}-\int\sum_{i=1}^{2}\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\partial_{x}R_{1}-\int\left(\partial_{x}S+T\right)\partial_{x}R_{1}.

We handle each terms on the right-hand side of the above identity separately. First, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int\epsilon\frac{d}{dt}\partial_{x}R_{1}\right|\lesssim\left(|\dot{\mu}_{1}|+|\dot{z}_{1}|+|\dot{\omega}_{1}|\right)\left\|\epsilon\right\|_{L^{2}}.

By integrating by parts and using
\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{x}R_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int\partial_{x}\left(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+2V\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}R_{1}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

On the one hand, we compute by using the orthogonality relations (
B.3
)

\displaystyle\int\overrightarrow{m_{1}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}\partial_{x}R_{1}=\left(-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}+\lambda_{1}\right)\left(1+\mu_{1}\right)\|\partial_{x}Q\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
C.4
that

\displaystyle\left|\int\overrightarrow{m_{2}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\partial_{x}R_{1}\right|\lesssim\left|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}\right|e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}.

Finally, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(\partial_{x}S+T\right)\partial_{x}R_{1}\right|\lesssim\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, we conclude gathering these estimates, and choosing
\sigma^{\star}
and
\nu_{1}^{\star}
small enough that

\displaystyle\left|-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}\right|\lesssim\left|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}\right|e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}+\left(1+|\dot{\mu}_{1}|+|\dot{z}_{1}|+|\dot{\omega}_{1}|\right)\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}.

Similar computations with the time derivative of the orthogonality relations
\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{1}=\int\epsilon\partial_{x}R_{2}=\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{2}=0
yield the estimates on
\dot{z}_{2}
,
\dot{\omega}_{1}
and
\dot{\omega_{2}}
in (
3.11
).

Next, by taking the time derivative of the orthogonality relation
\int\epsilon R_{1}=0
, and by using (
3.5
) and (
2.42
), we find that

\displaystyle 0=\int\epsilon\frac{d}{dt}R_{1}-\int\partial_{x}\left(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+2V\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)R_{1}-\int\sum_{i=1}^{2}\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}R_{1}-\int\partial_{x}SR_{1}-\int TR_{1}.

By using the orthogonality conditions
\int\epsilon\partial_{x}R_{1}=\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{1}=0
(see (
3.2
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int\epsilon\frac{d}{dt}R_{1}\right|=\left|\dot{\mu}_{1}\int\epsilon\Lambda R_{1}\right|\lesssim|\dot{\mu}_{1}|\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}.

Integration by parts, the second identity (
2.24
) and the orthogonality relation
\int\epsilon\partial_{x}R_{1}=0
implies that

\int\partial_{x}\left(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+2R_{1}\epsilon\right)R_{1}=-\mu_{1}\int\epsilon\partial_{x}R_{1}=0.

By using (
2.38
), it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\int 2\epsilon(V-R_{1})\partial_{x}R_{1}\right|+\left|\int\epsilon^{2}\partial_{x}R_{1}\right|\lesssim e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

On the one hand, the orthogonality relations (
B.3
) imply

\displaystyle\int\overrightarrow{m_{1}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}R_{1}=\left(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1}\right)\int\Lambda R_{1}R_{1}=\left(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1}\right)(1+\mu_{1})\int Q\Lambda Q.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
C.4
that

\displaystyle\left|\int\overrightarrow{m_{2}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}R_{1}\right|\lesssim|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}|e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}.

Gathering the previous inequalities and arguing similarly with the time derivative of the orthogonality relation
\int\epsilon R_{2}=0
yield (
3.12
).

Next, we deduce from (
3.11
)-(
3.12
) and the estimates in Proposition
2.4
the rough bound
\sum_{i=1}\left(|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{z}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)\lesssim 1
. By bootstraping this estimate into (
3.11
)-(
3.12
) and taking
Z_{1}^{\star}
large enough, we deduce that

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}},

(3.13)

which implies (
3.7
).

Finally, we obtain that
\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{\mu}_{i}|\lesssim e^{-\frac{7}{8}z}
, by combining the bounds (
3.12
) and (
3.13
) with (
2.45
)-(
2.50
). Therefore, we conclude the proof of (
3.8
) by reinjecting this estimate together with (
3.13
) into (
3.12
).
∎

3.3.
Energy estimates

In order to study the evolution of
\epsilon
, we follow the approach in
[
41
]
based on monotonicity formulas for localized portions of the mass and the energy. To this end, we use adequate weight functions
\psi_{+}
and
\psi_{-,m}
and
\psi_{-,e}
defined as follows. For a fixed
0<\rho<\frac{1}{32}
, we set

\displaystyle\psi(x):=\frac{2}{\pi}\arctan\left(e^{8\rho x}\right),

(3.14)

so that
\displaystyle\lim_{x\to-\infty}\psi(x)=0
,
\displaystyle\lim_{x\to+\infty}\psi(x)=1
, and , for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(x)=\frac{8\rho}{\pi\cosh(8\rho x)}>0,\quad 1-\psi(x)=\psi(-x),

(3.15)

\displaystyle|\psi^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq 8\rho\psi^{\prime}(x),\quad|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)|\leq(8\rho)^{2}\psi^{\prime}(x).

(3.16)

We define the three weight functions

\displaystyle\psi_{+}(t,x):=\mu_{1}(t)\psi(x)+\mu_{2}(t)\left(1-\psi(x)\right)

(3.17)

\displaystyle\psi_{-,e}(t,x):=\frac{\psi(x)}{\left(1+\mu_{1}(t)\right)^{2}}+\frac{1-\psi(x)}{\left(1+\mu_{2}(t)\right)^{2}},

(3.18)

\displaystyle\psi_{-,m}(t,x):=\frac{\mu_{1}(t)}{\left(1+\mu_{1}(t)\right)^{2}}\psi(x)+\frac{\mu_{2}(t)}{\left(1+\mu_{2}(t)\right)^{2}}\left(1-\psi(x)\right)

(3.19)

The following properties of the weight functions are deduced from (
3.14
)-(
3.16
).

Claim 3.6
.

(i)

Assume
t\in I
is such that
\mu_{1}(t)\geq\mu_{2}(t)
. Then we have for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\partial_{x}\psi_{+}(t,x)=\mu(t)\psi^{\prime}(x)\geq 0;\quad|\partial_{x}^{2}\psi_{+}(t,x)|\leq\frac{1}{4}\partial_{x}\psi_{+}(t,x);\quad|\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{+}(t,x)|\leq\frac{1}{16}\partial_{x}\psi_{+}(t,x).

(3.20)

(ii)

Assume
t\in I
is such that
\mu_{2}(t)\geq\mu_{1}(t)
. Then we have for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}(t,x)=-\mu(t)\frac{2+\mu_{1}(t)+\mu_{2}(t)}{(1+\mu_{1}(t))^{2}(1+\mu_{2}(t))^{2}}\psi^{\prime}(x)\geq 0,

(3.21)

\displaystyle 1-\psi_{-,e}(t,x)=\mu_{1}(t)\frac{2+\mu_{1}(t)}{(1+\mu_{1}(t))^{2}}\psi(x)+\mu_{2}(t)\frac{2+\mu_{2}(t)}{(1+\mu_{2}(t))^{2}}(1-\psi(x)),

(3.22)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{2}\psi_{-,e}(t,x)|\leq\frac{1}{4}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}(t,x)\quad\text{and}\quad|\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{-,e}(t,x)|\leq\frac{1}{16}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}(t,x).

(3.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}(t,x)=\mu(t)\frac{1-\mu_{1}(t)\mu_{2}(t)}{(1+\mu_{1}(t))^{2}(1+\mu_{2}(t))^{2}}\psi^{\prime}(x)\leq 0;

(3.24)

We define the localized mass-energy functionals

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{+}

\displaystyle:=\int\left(\frac{|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{3}\left((V+\epsilon)^{3}-V^{3}-3V^{2}\epsilon\right)\right)+\int\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\psi_{+}-\int S\epsilon

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{-}

\displaystyle:=\int\left(\frac{|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{3}\left((V+\epsilon)^{3}-V^{3}-3V^{2}\epsilon\right)\right)\psi_{-,e}+\int\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\psi_{-,m}-\int S\epsilon,

(3.26)

where
S
is given in the decomposition of
\mathcal{E}_{V}
in (
2.42
).

Remark 3.7
.

Following
[
41
]
, the mass terms
\int\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\psi_{+}
, respectively
\int\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\psi_{-,m}
are introduced in
\mathcal{F}_{+}
, respectively
\mathcal{F}_{-}
, to cancel out some bad contributions involving the time derivative
\partial_{t}V
of the approximate solution
V
. In the case where
\mu_{2}\geq\mu_{1}
,
\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}
has a bad sign (see (
3.24
)), and therefore the energy needs also to be localised to compensate some bad contributions coming from the localised mass.

Remark 3.8
.

Compared to
[
41
]
, the energy functional is now defined with the addition of the term
\int S\epsilon
, which will help to cancel out the main part of the contribution coming from the error term
\mathcal{E}_{V}
when computing the time derivative of
\mathcal{F}_{+}
and
\mathcal{F}_{-}
. A similar idea was used by Martel and Nguyen in
[
44
]
to study strong interactions for a
1
-dimensional system of cubic Schrödinger equations (see the term
\mathbf{S}
in Section
5
of
[
44
]
).

Next, we state some useful estimates involving the approximate solution
V
and the weight functions, and whose proof is given in Appendix
C
.

Claim 3.9
.

For
i=1,2
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\||\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}|\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim e^{-\rho z};

(3.27)

\displaystyle\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim e^{-\rho z};

(3.28)

\displaystyle\|V\psi^{\prime}\|_{H^{1}}+\|V\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{x}V\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim e^{-\rho z};

(3.29)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}V+\partial_{x}V\psi_{+}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|\dot{z}_{i}-\mu_{i}|+|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)e^{-\rho z}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{z}_{i}|;

(3.30)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}V\psi_{-,e}+\partial_{x}V\psi_{-,m}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|\dot{z}_{i}-\mu_{i}|+|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)+e^{-\rho z}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|+|\dot{z}_{i}|\right).

(3.31)

Before giving some precise estimates for the time derivative of
\mathcal{F}_{+}
and
\mathcal{F}_{-}
, we introduce a time-dependant quantity which will be useful to express these bounds:

\displaystyle\Theta:=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int S\left(\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\right)\right|+\|S\|_{L^{2}}\|T\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.32)

Proposition 3.10
.

The following estimates hold true.

(i)

Coercivity
. For all
t\in I
,

\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{F}_{+}+\|S\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{F}_{-}+\|S\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

(3.33)

(ii)

If
t\in I
is such that
\mu_{1}(t)\geq\mu_{2}(t)
, then

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{+}&\lesssim\Theta+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\|S\|_{H^{1}}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}S\|_{L^{2}}\right)\\
&\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)e^{-\rho z}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\right).\end{split}

(3.34)

(iii)

If
t\in I
is such that
\mu_{2}(t)\geq\mu_{1}(t)
, then

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{-}&\lesssim\Theta+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\|S\|_{H^{2}}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}S\|_{L^{2}}\right)\\
&\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)e^{-\rho z}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\right).\end{split}

(3.35)

Proof.

Proof of estimate (
3.33
).
First, for
\mathcal{F}_{-}
, we use a localization argument (See Appendix A of
[
36
]
and Appendix A of
[
58
]
) to obtain almost orthogonality relations. Next, around each
R_{i}
, the operator in the first integral of
\mathcal{F}_{+}
and
\mathcal{F}_{-}
essentially behaves like the linearized operator
L_{\tilde{R}_{i}}
. From the coercivity property of
L
under suitable orthogonality relations (see Proposition
2.1
(iv)), we obtain the existence of a constant
\lambda>0
such that, for any
\epsilon
satisfying (
3.2
), it holds

\displaystyle\lambda\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq\int\frac{|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}-R_{i}\epsilon^{2}.

Notice finally that
|\psi_{+}|+|\psi_{-,e}|\lesssim|\mu_{1}|+|\mu_{2}|
by (
3.17
). Decreasing
\lambda
if necessary, estimates (
3.33
) follow by choosing
\sigma^{\star}\gtrsim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}
and
\nu_{1}^{\star}>|\mu_{1}|+|\mu_{2}|
small enough and using Young’s inequality.

Proof of estimate (
3.34
)

We decompose the time derivative of
\mathcal{F}_{+}
as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{+}

\displaystyle=f_{1}^{+}+f_{2}^{+}+f_{3}^{+},

where

\displaystyle f_{1}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\int\partial_{t}\epsilon\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)-\int S\partial_{t}\epsilon,

\displaystyle f_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle:=-\int\partial_{t}V\epsilon^{2}+\int(\partial_{t}\epsilon)\epsilon\psi_{+},

\displaystyle f_{3}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\int\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{+}-\int\partial_{t}S\epsilon.

Estimate for
f_{1}^{+}
.
We claim that

\left|f_{1}^{+}\right|\lesssim\Theta+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i})\right|+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|+\|T\|_{H^{1}}\right)+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|.

(3.36)

By the equation satisfied by
\epsilon
in (
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle f_{1}^{+}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int\left(-\Delta+1-2R_{i}\right)\left(\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\right)\epsilon+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\left(2(V-R_{i})\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\int T\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)+\int S\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}+T\right)

\displaystyle=:f^{+}_{1,1}+f^{+}_{1,2}+f^{+}_{1,3}+f^{+}_{1,4}.

It follows from the second identity in (
2.24
) and the orthogonality relations (
3.2
) that

\displaystyle|f_{1,1}^{+}|\lesssim\left|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int\left(-\Delta+1-2R_{i}\right)\left(\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\right)\epsilon\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i})\right|.

Next, we use (
2.38
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\left|f_{1,2}^{+}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|\left(e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

By using the Hölder inequality and the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and recalling that
\|V\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
, we find

\displaystyle\left|f_{1,3}^{+}\right|\lesssim\|T\|_{H^{1}}\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}+\|T\|_{L^{3}}\|\epsilon\|_{L^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\|T\|_{H^{1}}\left(\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\lesssim\|T\|_{H^{1}}\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}.

Finally, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields

\left|f_{1,4}^{+}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int S\left(\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\right)\right|+\|S\|_{L^{2}}\|T\|_{L^{2}}.

The proof of (
3.36
) follows gathering these estimates and the definition of
\Theta
in (
3.32
).

Estimate for
f_{2}^{+}
. We claim that

\begin{split}f_{2}^{+}&+\frac{1}{4}\int\left((\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{+}\\
&\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(|\mu|\left(|\overrightarrow{m_{1}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\|_{L^{2}}+|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}\left(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right)\right|\right)\\
&\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left(\|\partial_{t}V+\partial_{x}V\psi_{+}\|_{H^{1}}+\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{+}\|_{H^{1}}\right).\end{split}

(3.37)

Indeed, by using the equation for
\epsilon
and integrating by parts, we rearrange the term
f_{2}^{+}
as

\displaystyle f_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle=-\int\left(\partial_{t}V+\partial_{x}V\psi_{+}\right)\epsilon^{2}+\int\epsilon^{2}V\partial_{x}\psi_{+}-\int\partial_{x}\left(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\epsilon\psi_{+}-\int\mathcal{E}_{V}\epsilon\psi_{+}

\displaystyle=:f_{2,1}^{+}+f_{2,2}^{+}+f_{2,3}^{+}+f_{2,4}^{+}.

First, observe from Hölder’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\left|f_{2,1}^{+}\right|+\left|f_{2,2}^{+}\right|\lesssim\left(\|\partial_{t}V+\partial_{x}V\psi_{+}\|_{H^{1}}+\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{+}\|_{H^{1}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Next, after integration by parts, we rewrite

\displaystyle f_{2,3}^{+}=-\frac{1}{2}\int\left(3(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{+}+\frac{2}{3}\int\epsilon^{3}\partial_{x}\psi_{+}+\frac{1}{2}\int\epsilon^{2}\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{+}.

By using Hölder’s inequality, the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and (
3.20
), we observe that

\int\epsilon^{3}\partial_{x}\psi_{+}\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{3}}\|\epsilon\sqrt{\partial_{x}\psi_{+}}\|_{L^{3}}^{2}\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\int\left(\epsilon^{2}+(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{+}.

Then, we deduce by using (
3.20
) and choosing
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sigma^{\star}
small enough that

f_{2,3}^{+}+\frac{1}{4}\int\left((\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{+}\leq 0.

Finally, we deal with the last term of
f_{2}^{+}
. From the decomposition of the error
\mathcal{E}_{V}
in (
2.42
) and the definition of
\psi_{+}
in (
3.17
), we have

\displaystyle\left|f_{2,4}^{+}\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\epsilon\psi_{+}\right|+\left(\|S\|_{H^{1}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|.

Using the orthogonality conditions (
3.2
) and the second identity in (
2.24
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int\overrightarrow{m_{1}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}\epsilon\psi_{+}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\overrightarrow{m_{1}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}\epsilon\mu(\psi-1)\right|+|\mu_{1}(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1})|\left|\int\Lambda R_{1}\epsilon\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\mu||\overrightarrow{m_{1}}|\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\|_{L^{2}}+\left|\mu_{1}\right|\left|\left(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1}\right)\right|\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}

and thus, we conclude arguing similarly for the term
\left|\int\overrightarrow{m_{2}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\epsilon\psi_{+}\right|
that

\displaystyle\left|f_{2,4}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(|\mu|\left(|\overrightarrow{m_{1}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\|_{L^{2}}+|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}\left(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|.

Hence, the proof of (
3.37
) follows by gathering the above estimates.

Estimate for
f_{3}^{+}
. From the definition of
\psi_{+}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we infer that

\displaystyle\left|f_{3}^{+}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}S\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.38)

Therefore, we conclude the proof of estimate (
3.34
) by combining (
3.36
), (
3.37
), (
3.38
) with (
3.7
)-(
3.8
), (
3.10
) and (
3.28
)-(
3.29
)-(
3.30
).

Proof of estimate (
3.35
)
. After integrating by parts, we rearrange the time derivative of
\mathcal{F}_{-}
as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{-}

\displaystyle=f_{1}^{-}+f_{2}^{-}+f_{3}^{-}+f_{4}^{-},

where

\displaystyle f_{1}^{-}

\displaystyle:=\int\partial_{t}\epsilon\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)-\int S\partial_{t}\epsilon;

\displaystyle f_{2}^{-}

\displaystyle:=\int\partial_{t}\epsilon\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)(\psi_{-,e}-1)-\int\partial_{t}\epsilon\partial_{x}\epsilon\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e};

\displaystyle f_{3}^{-}

\displaystyle:=-\int\partial_{t}V\epsilon^{2}\psi_{-,e}+\int(\partial_{t}\epsilon)\epsilon\psi_{-,m};

\displaystyle f_{4}^{-}

\displaystyle:=\int\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}\psi_{-,m}-\int\partial_{t}S\epsilon+\int\left(\frac{|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{3}\left(3V\epsilon^{2}+\epsilon^{3}\right)\right)\partial_{t}\psi_{-,e}.

Estimate for
f_{1}^{-}
. Observe that
f_{1}^{-}=f_{1}^{+}
, so that we deduce from (
3.36
) that

\left|f_{1}^{-}\right|\lesssim\Theta+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i})\right|+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|+\|T\|_{H^{1}}\right)+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|.

(3.39)

Estimate for
f_{2}^{-}
. We claim that

\begin{split}f_{2}^{-}&+\frac{3}{8}\int\left(|\partial_{x}\nabla\epsilon|^{2}+3(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\\
&\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left|\mu_{i}\left(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right)\right|+|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|e^{-\rho z}\right)+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{H^{1}}+\|T\|_{H^{1}}\right)\\
&\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|+e^{-\rho z}\right).\end{split}

(3.40)

Indeed, by using the equation (
3.5
) satisfied by
\epsilon
and performing an integration by parts, we rewrite
f_{2}^{-}
as

\displaystyle f_{2}^{-}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}-\int(\partial_{x}\epsilon)\partial_{x}\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}

\displaystyle\quad-\int\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-(V+\epsilon)^{2}+V^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}_{V}(\psi_{-,e}-1)+\int(\partial_{x}\epsilon)\mathcal{E}_{V}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}

\displaystyle=:f_{2,1}^{-}+f_{2,2}^{-}+f_{2,3}^{-}+f_{2,4}^{-}.

First, observe from Young’s inequality that

f_{2,1}^{-}\leq-\frac{7}{16}\int\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon\right)^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}+C\int\left(V^{2}\epsilon^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}.

On the one hand, more integrations by parts yield

\displaystyle-\frac{7}{16}\int\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon\right)^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}

\displaystyle=-\frac{7}{16}\int\left((-\Delta\epsilon)^{2}+2|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}+\frac{7}{16}\int\epsilon^{2}\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{-,e}.

On the other hand, we deduce from Hölder’s inequality, the Sobolev embedding and (
3.23
) that

\displaystyle\int V^{2}\epsilon^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{H^{1}}\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2},

\displaystyle\int\epsilon^{4}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\epsilon\sqrt{\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\int\left(|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}.

Hence, it follows by using (
3.23
) and taking
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sigma^{\star}
small enough that

f_{2,1}^{-}+\frac{3}{8}\int\left((-\Delta\epsilon)^{2}+2|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\lesssim\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{H^{1}}\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

We argue similarly for
f_{2,2}^{-}
. After some integration by parts, we obtain that

\displaystyle f_{2,2}^{-}

\displaystyle=-\int\left((\partial_{x}^{2}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{xy}^{2}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}+\frac{1}{2}\int(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{-,e}+2\int\epsilon(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}

\displaystyle\quad+2\int(\partial_{x}\epsilon)\partial_{x}(V\epsilon)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}.

We deduce from Hölder’s inequality, the Sobolev embedding and (
3.23
) that

\displaystyle\left|\int(\partial_{x}\epsilon)\partial_{x}(V\epsilon)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\right|\lesssim\left(\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{x}V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\int\epsilon(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\|\partial_{x}\epsilon\sqrt{\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\int\left((\partial_{x}^{2}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{xy}^{2}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}.

Hence, it follows by using (
3.23
) and taking
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sigma^{\star}
small enough that

f_{2,2}^{-}+\frac{1}{2}\int\left((\partial_{x}^{2}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{xy}^{2}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\lesssim\left(\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{x}V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Next, we deal with the term
f_{2,3}^{-}
. Recall the notation of
L_{R_{i}}
in (
2.17
). We infer from Hölder’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\left|f_{2,3}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-2R_{i}\epsilon)\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}(\psi_{-,e}-1)\right|+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int\left(V-R_{i}\right)\overrightarrow{m_{i}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\epsilon\left(\psi_{-,e}-1\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\|\mathcal{E}_{V}\|_{H^{1}}\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\|\partial_{x}S\|_{H^{1}}+\|T\|_{H^{1}}\right)\right)\left(\|\psi_{-,e}-1\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right).

Using the orthogonality conditions (
3.2
), the second identity in (
2.24
) and (
3.22
), we have that

\displaystyle\left|\int(-\Delta\epsilon\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\epsilon-2R_{1}\epsilon)\overrightarrow{m_{1}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi_{-,e}-1)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left|\int(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-2R_{1}\epsilon)\overrightarrow{m_{1}}\cdot\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\right|+\mu_{1}^{2}|(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1})|\left|\int\Lambda R_{1}\epsilon\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}|\overrightarrow{m_{1}}|\left\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(1-\psi)\right\|_{H^{1}}+\mu_{1}^{2}|\left(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1}\right)|\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}.

We argue similarly for the term involving
\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}
. Therefore, by using
\|\partial_{x}\psi_{e,-}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\psi_{e,-}-1\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|
, we conclude that

\displaystyle|f_{2,3}^{-}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left(|\overrightarrow{m_{1}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\|_{L^{2}}+|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\psi\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}\left(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|\left\|(V-R_{i})\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{H^{1}}+\|T\|_{H^{1}}+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left\|\mathcal{E}_{V}\right\|_{H^{1}}\right).

Finally, we estimate the term
f_{2,4}^{-}
by using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the identity (
3.21
). More precisely, we have

|f_{2,4}^{-}|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Therefore, we conclude the proof of (
3.40
) by combining these estimates with (
2.38
), (
3.27
), (
3.28
) and (
3.29
).

Estimate for
f_{3}^{-}
. We claim that

\begin{split}|f_{3}^{-}|&\lesssim\frac{5}{16}\int\left(3(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}\\
&\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}e^{-\rho z}|\overrightarrow{m_{i}}|+\|S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}\left(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right)\right|\right)\\
&\quad+\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|\dot{z}_{i}-\mu_{i}|+|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|+|\dot{z}_{i}|\right)e^{-\rho z}\right).\end{split}

(3.41)

By using the equation of
\epsilon
in (
3.5
), we decompose
f_{3}^{-}
as

\displaystyle f_{3}^{-}

\displaystyle=-\int\left(\partial_{t}V\psi_{-,e}+\partial_{x}V\psi_{-,m}\right)\epsilon^{2}+\int V\epsilon^{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}-\int\partial_{x}\left(\Delta\epsilon-\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\epsilon\psi_{-,m}-\int\mathcal{E}_{V}\epsilon\psi_{-,m}

\displaystyle=:f_{3,1}^{-}+f_{3,2}^{-}+f_{3,3}^{-}+f_{3,4}^{-}.

Firstly, it follows from Hölder’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding that

|f_{3,1}^{-}|+|f_{3,2}^{-}|\lesssim\left(\|\partial_{t}V\psi_{-,e}+\partial_{x}V\psi_{-,m}\|_{H^{1}}+\|V\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}\|_{H^{1}}\right)\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Secondly, we rewrite after integrating by parts

f_{3,3}^{-}=-\frac{1}{2}\int\left(3(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}+\frac{1}{2}\int\epsilon^{2}\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{-,m}+\frac{2}{3}\int\epsilon^{3}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}

Note that the first term on the right-hand side of the above identity has a bad sign since
\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}\leq 0
. However, it follows from (
3.21
) and (
3.24
) that
|\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}|+|\partial_{x}^{3}\psi_{-,m}|\leq\frac{9}{16}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}
. Moreover, we deduce from Hölder’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding that

\left|\int\epsilon^{3}\partial_{x}\psi_{-,m}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\int\left(|\nabla\epsilon|^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e}.

Hence, we have, recalling that
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sigma^{\star}
is chosen small enough,

|f_{3,3}^{-}|\leq\frac{5}{16}\int\left(3(\partial_{x}\epsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y}\epsilon)^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{x}\psi_{-,e},

so that the contribution of
|f_{3,3}^{-}|
will be absorbed by the second term on the left-hand side of (
3.40
). Thirdly, we bound the contribution
|f_{3,4}^{-}|
arguing as with
|f_{2,4}^{+}|
. More precisely, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|f_{3,4}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\left(|\mu|\left(|\overrightarrow{m_{1}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{1}}(\psi-1)\|_{L^{2}}+|\overrightarrow{m_{2}}|\|\overrightarrow{MR_{2}}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}\left(\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left\|\epsilon\right\|_{H^{1}}\left(\|S\|_{L^{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\mu_{i}|.

Therefore, we conclude the proof of (
3.41
) by combining the above estimates with (
3.28
), (
3.29
) and (
3.31
).

Estimate for
f_{4}^{-}
.
We infer from Hölder’s inequality, the Sobolev embedding and the definitions of
\psi_{-,e}
and
\psi_{-,m}
that

\displaystyle\left|f_{4}^{-}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}S\|_{L^{2}},

(3.42)

where we also used that
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\sigma^{\star}
, where
\sigma^{\star}
is chosen small enough.

Therefore, we conclude the proof of (
3.35
) by combining (
3.39
)-(
3.42
) with (
3.7
)-(
3.8
), and (
3.9
)-(
3.10
).
∎

4.
Stability of the collision of two nearly equal solitary waves

In this section, we study the evolution of a solution close to the approximate solution
V
constructed in Section
2
. Before studying the stability of two solitary-waves, we introduce in the first subsection a function
Z
which approximates the distance between the two solitary waves. Then, we state the main stability result in Subsection
4.2
, which is proved in the following subsections.

4.1.
The function
Z

We gather below several properties of the function
Z
that are proved in Appendix
D
.

Lemma 4.1

(Pointwise properties of
Z
)
.

Let
0<\mu_{0}\leq\left(2\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle^{-1}\|Q\|_{L^{3}}^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. There exists a unique even function
Z
in
\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

\displaystyle\ddot{Z}(t)=\frac{2}{\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q(x+Z(t),y)\partial_{x}(Q^{2})(x,y)dxdy,

(4.1)

with the condition

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}(Z(t),\dot{Z}(t))=(+\infty,-2\mu_{0}).

Furthermore,
Z
satisfies the following properties.

(i)

(Convexity)
Z
is a convex function with a positive minimum at
0
.

(ii)

(Asymptote) There exist a real constant
l=l(\mu_{0})\in\mathbb{R}
and some positive constants
c=c(\mu_{0})
and
K=K(\mu_{0})
such that for
t<0

\displaystyle\left|Z(t)+2\mu_{0}t-l\right|\leq Ke^{ct}.

(4.2)

Proof.

The existence and uniqueness of
Z
are proved in Corollary
D.4
and Lemma
D.5
. The proof of existence and the value of the asymptote is given in Lemma
D.5
.
∎

Lemma 4.2

(Uniform estimates on
Z
)
.

There exist
\nu_{2}^{\star}>0
and two positive constants
c
and
C
such that for any
0<\mu_{0}<\nu_{2}^{\star}
,
Z
defined as in Lemma
4.1
, the following holds true.

(i)

(Behaviour at
0
) Let
Z_{0}:=Z(0)
, then
\mu_{0}\sim Z_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}Z_{0}}
. More precisely

\displaystyle cZ_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z_{0}}\leq\mu_{0}^{2}\leq CZ_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z_{0}}.

(4.3)

(ii)

(Large time
T_{1}
) Let
T_{1}
be the unique positive time such that

\displaystyle Z(T_{1})=\rho^{-1}Z_{0},

(4.4)

where
0<\rho<\frac{1}{32}
has been fixed in Section
3.3
. Then

\displaystyle\dot{Z}(T_{1})\mu_{0}^{-1}\geq 1.

(4.5)

Proof.

The first point is proved in Lemma
D.8
. Second, from (
D.7
) with
l_{1}=2\mu_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\dot{Z}(T_{1})^{2}=4\mu_{0}^{2}-\frac{4}{\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle}\int Q(\mathbf{x}-(Z(T_{1}),0))Q^{2}(\mathbf{x})d\mathbf{x},

so that, by using Proposition
D.6
, Lemma
D.8
and
\frac{2}{\rho}>10
,

\displaystyle\left|\dot{Z}(T_{1})^{2}-4\mu_{0}^{2}\right|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{\rho}Z_{0}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{10}.

(4.6)

Hence, choosing
\nu_{2}^{\star}
small enough provides the desired inequality.
∎

4.2.
Dynamics of a solution close to a
2
-solitary waves structure in the collision region

In this subsection, we describe the behaviour of a solution close to the approximate solution
V
constructed in Section
2
in the collision region. The main result of this section is stated below.

We recall that
0<\rho<\frac{1}{32}
has been defined in Section
3.3
. For
\mu_{0}>0
small, let
Z
as defined in Lemma
4.1
and define
Z_{0}:=Z(0)
. Recall also from (
4.3
) that
\mu_{0}\sim Z_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}Z_{0}}
.

Proposition 4.3
.

There exist some constants
C>0
and
\nu_{3}^{\star}
such that the following holds. Let
0<\mu_{0}<\nu_{3}^{\star}
,
|\omega_{0}|<\mu_{0}
,
w^{1}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\Gamma^{1}=(z_{1}^{1},z_{2}^{1},\omega_{1}^{1},\omega_{2}^{1},\mu_{1}^{1},\mu_{2}^{1})
satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|w^{1}-V(\Gamma_{1})\right\|_{H^{1}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\mu_{i}^{1}+\frac{(-1)^{i}}{2}\dot{Z}(-T_{1})\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-4}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|z_{i}^{1}+\frac{(-1)^{i}}{2}Z(-T_{1})\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\omega_{i}^{1}+\frac{(-1)^{i}}{2}\omega_{0}\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}.

By denoting
w
the solution to (
1.10
) with initial condition
w(-T_{1})=w^{1}
, there exists
(\Gamma(t),\epsilon(t))\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
such that for any
t\in[-T_{1},T_{1}]

\displaystyle w(t,\mathbf{x})=V(t,\mathbf{x})+\epsilon(t,\mathbf{x}),\quad\Gamma(t)=\left(z_{1}(t),z_{2}(t),\omega_{1}(t),\omega_{2}(t),\mu_{1}(t),\mu_{2}(t)\right),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\epsilon(t)\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},&&\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|\leq CZ_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},&&\left|\bar{\mu}(t)\right|\leq C\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},\\
&\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|\leq CZ_{0}^{2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},&&\left|\omega(t)-\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\bar{\omega}(t)\right|\leq CZ_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}},&&\left|\bar{z}(t)\right|\leq CZ_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.\end{aligned}

(4.8)

where
z(t)
,
\bar{z}(t)
,
\omega(t)
,
\bar{\omega}(t)
,
\mu(t)
and
\bar{\mu}(t)
are defined in (
2.10
), (
2.11
) and (
2.12
).

The rest of this section is dedicated to the proof of this proposition. Let us begin with an overview of the proof.

The two solitary waves exchange their mass on the time interval
[-T_{1},T_{1}]
. In particular, at the “switching time”, the solitary wave on the left becomes the smallest one and after the collision heads to the left, whereas the one on the right becomes the highest one and after the collision heads to the right. To describe this whole phenomenon in the region
[-T_{1},T_{1}]
, we split the time interval into three regions based on a fixed time
T_{2}
to define later.

•

In the first region
[-T_{1},-T_{2}]
, the solitary wave on the left is higher than the one on the right. The estimates on the error terms are optimal in the sense that they cannot be improved significantly due to the error of approximation between
w
and
V(\Gamma)
.

•

In the second region
[-T_{2},T_{2}]
, a small interval around
0
, the smallest solitary wave becomes the highest. The estimates on the different parameters are based on the smallness of the interval.

•

In the third region
[T_{2},T_{1}]
, we deal with the two solitary waves going away one from another. We thus integrate the accumulated error term on an interval of size
T_{1}-T_{2}
, until the two solitary waves are far enough one from another to enter the orbital stability regime.

Throughout these parts, we will need our solution to satisfy Assumption
2.3
and to use Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
. Lemmata
D.10
and
D.14
will be used to estimate the parameters controlled by an approximate ODE system. In that context, by using the constant
C
as defined in Lemma
D.8
, we fix the following constraint

\displaystyle\nu_{3}^{\star}\leq\frac{1}{2}\min\left(\sigma^{\star},\nu_{1}^{\star},C(Z_{1}^{\star})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z_{1}^{\star}},\nu_{2}^{\star},\nu_{6}^{\star}\right).

(4.9)

As long as
w(t)
stays close to the approximation
V
, we introduce the decomposition
(\Gamma(t),\epsilon(t))
as constructed in Lemma
3.1
. In particular, we have from (
4.7
) at time
t=-T_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\epsilon(-T_{1})\right\|_{H^{1}}+\left|\mu(-T_{1})-\dot{Z}(-T_{1})\right|+\left|\bar{\mu}(-T_{1})\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}

(4.10)

\displaystyle\left|z(-T_{1})-Z(-T_{1})\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\left|\bar{z}(-T_{1})\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\left|\omega(-T_{1})-\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\bar{\omega}(-T_{1})\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{-}(-T_{1})\leq Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}.

(4.12)

Following the previous discussion on splitting
[-T_{1},T_{1}]
into different regions, we define, for a parameter
\eta
small to be fixed later, the time
T_{2}
as the unique positive time satisfying

\displaystyle Z(T_{2})=Z_{0}+\eta^{2},

(4.13)

and the time
T_{3}
as the unique time in
(T_{2},T_{1})
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{0}^{2}=Z_{0}Z(T_{3})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(T_{3})}.

(4.14)

From Lemmata
4.2
and
D.9
we have

\displaystyle\dot{Z}(T_{1})\mu_{0}^{-1}\geq 1\quad\text{and}\quad\dot{Z}(T_{2})\gtrsim\eta\mu_{0}.

(4.15)

Let
C_{1}>1
to be chosen later, and consider the bootstrap estimates on each of the two regions:

•

For any
t\in[-T_{1},T_{2}]
,

\|\epsilon(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\left|\bar{\mu}(t)\right|\leq\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\left|\bar{z}(t)\right|\leq\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\left|\omega(t)-\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\bar{\omega}(t)\right|\leq\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}.

(4.16)

–

for any
t\in[-T_{1},-T_{3}]

\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|\leq Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(4.17)

–

for any
t\in[-T_{3},T_{2}]

\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|\leq\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|\leq\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(4.18)

•

For any
t\in[T_{2},T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\epsilon(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\left|\bar{\mu}(t)\right|\leq C_{1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\left|\bar{z}(t)\right|\leq C_{1}Z_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\left|\omega(t)-\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\bar{\omega}(t)\right|\leq C_{1}Z_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}

(4.19)

\displaystyle\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|\leq C_{1}Z_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|\leq C_{1}Z_{0}^{2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}},.

We prove the proposition by a bootstrap argument. Let us define the maximal time
T^{\star}
so that the bootstrap estimates are satisfied

T^{\star}:=\sup\left\{t\in[-T_{1};T_{1}]\text{ such that }\eqref{est:-T_1_T_1}\text{ and }\eqref{bootstrap_-T_1}-\eqref{bootstrap_T_2}\text{ hold on }[-T_{1};t)\right\}.

Note first that estimates (
4.16
)-(
4.17
) hold at time
-T_{1}
by (
4.10
)-(
4.11
). By continuity of
t\mapsto w(t)
in
H^{1}
and of
\Gamma
,
T^{\star}
is well-defined.

Now, we aim at proving that
T^{\star}=T_{1}
by choosing
C_{1}
large enough (independent of
\mu_{0}
) and
\mu_{0}<\nu_{4}^{\star}
small enough (possibly depending on
C_{1}
). We argue by contradiction by assuming
T^{\star}<T_{1}
. The proof of Proposition
4.3
is given in the next subsections.

4.3.
Estimates in the bootstrap setting

Since (
4.16
)-(
4.19
) hold on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
, it follows from the mean value theorem and by choosing
\nu_{4}^{\star}
small enough that

\displaystyle\left|z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-z(t)}-Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}\right|\lesssim\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}.

(4.20)

Moreover, as a consequence of Proposition
2.4
we deduce the following inequalities on
t\in[-T_{1},T^{\star})

\displaystyle\left\|S(t)\right\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)},

(4.21)

\displaystyle\left\|T(t)\right\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\mu_{0}Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{z}_{i}|,

(4.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\langle S(t),\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}(t)\rangle\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)},

(4.23)

Note that the implicit constants appearing in the former estimates are independent of
C_{1}
and
Z_{0}
. It will be the case for all the estimates in the rest of this section.

Next, we reformulate the estimates on the derivatives of the geometrical parameters obtained in Proposition
3.3
in the bootstrap setting.

Lemma 4.4

(Simplified dynamical system)
.

Recall the notations of
z
,
\bar{z}
,
\omega
,
\bar{\omega}
,
\mu
and
\bar{\mu}
in (
2.10
)-(
2.12
). The following system holds on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})

\displaystyle\left|\dot{z}-\mu\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{z}}-\bar{\mu}\right|+\left|\dot{\omega}\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{\omega}}\right|\lesssim Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}},

(4.24)

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\mu}+2\gamma_{1}(z)\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{\mu}}\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(4.25)

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left|\dot{z}_{1}\right|+\left|\dot{z}_{2}\right|+\left|\mu_{1}\right|+\left|\mu_{2}\right|+\left|\mu\right|+|\omega_{1}|+|\omega_{2}|\lesssim\mu_{0}.

(4.26)

Proof.

From Proposition
3.3
, (
2.41
), (
4.3
), (
4.21
)-(
4.23
) and
|\mu_{i}|\lesssim|\dot{Z}(t)|+\mu_{0}\lesssim\mu_{0}
(see Appendix
D
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left|-\dot{z}_{i}+\mu_{i}\right|+\left|\dot{\omega_{i}}\right|\right)\lesssim Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\text{ and }\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

The proof of (
4.24
)-(
4.25
) ends with the definition of the parameters in (
2.10
)-(
2.12
) and with (
2.40
) that ensures
\gamma_{1}(z)=-\gamma_{2}(z)
.

We now continue with the inequality on
\dot{z}_{1}
. From (
2.10
), we have

\displaystyle 2\left|\dot{z}_{1}\right|\leq\left|\dot{z}\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{z}}\right|\leq\left|\dot{z}-\mu\right|+\left|\mu-\dot{Z}\right|+\left|\dot{Z}\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{z}}-\bar{\mu}\right|+\left|\bar{\mu}\right|

which implies (
4.26
) by using (
4.24
), the estimates on
\mu-\dot{Z}
and
\bar{\mu}
in (
4.17
)-(
4.19
) and
|\dot{Z}(t)|\lesssim\mu_{0}
. We proceed similarly to prove the inequalities on
\dot{z}_{2}
,
\mu_{1}
,
\mu_{2}
and
\mu
in (
4.26
). The estimates on
\omega_{i}
follow from (
4.8
) and the assumption that
|\omega_{0}|\leq\mu_{0}
in Proposition
4.3

∎

Gathering (
2.49
), (
4.21
)-(
4.23
) and Lemma
4.4
, we obtain simplified estimates on the source terms:

\displaystyle\left\|S(t)\right\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)},

(4.27)

\displaystyle\left\|T(t)\right\|_{H^{1}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\langle S(t),\overrightarrow{MR_{i}}(t)\rangle\right|+\left\|\partial_{t}S(t)\right\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}.

(4.28)

Finally, we derive a refined estimate for the control of the derivative of the transverse translation parameter
\dot{\omega}_{i}
.
To this aim, we introduce the quantity

K_{i}(t,\mathbf{x})=\tilde{K}_{i}(t,\mathbf{x})\chi_{\mu_{0}}(x)\quad\text{with}\quad\widetilde{K}_{i}(t,\mathbf{x})=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\partial_{y}R_{i}(t,\tilde{x},y)d\tilde{x},

(4.29)

where
\chi_{\mu_{0}}(x)=\chi(\mu_{0}x)
and
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies
0\leq\chi\leq 1
,
\chi(x)=1
for
x\leq 1
and
\chi(x)=0
for
x\geq 2
. Note in particular that

\|{\bf{1}}_{(-\mu_{0}^{-1},+\infty)}\chi_{\mu_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.30)

Then, we deduce from the pointwise bounds on
\left|\partial_{y}Q(\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim e^{-|\mathbf{x}|}
(see Proposition
B.5
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\|K_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1\quad\text{and}\quad\|K_{i}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.31)

We define the quantity

\mathcal{K}_{i}(t)=\int\epsilon(t,\mathbf{x})K_{i}(t,\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}.

(4.32)

Proposition 4.5
.

It holds, for all
t\in[-T_{1},T^{\star})
and
j=1,2
,

\|\mathcal{K}_{i}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}

(4.33)

and

\begin{split}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{K}_{1}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|&+\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{K}_{2}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}-2c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\\
&\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|T\|_{L^{2}}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|S\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int S\partial_{y}R_{i}\right|\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\left|-\dot{z}_{i}+\mu_{i}+\alpha_{i}\right|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)\left(e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\mu_{0}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}|,\end{split}

(4.34)

where
c_{Q}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{y}\left(\int_{x}\partial_{y}Q(x,y)dx\right)^{2}dy>0
.

Remark 4.6
.

The functions
K_{i}
, used to improve the equation on
\dot{\omega}_{i}
, are an adaptation of the functions
J_{i}
introduced in Section 5.1 of
[
41
]
to the transverse direction. Note however that here, instead of using the decay on the right of the particular solution
v
defined in (
5.3
), we choose to cut the functions
K_{i}
on the right, so that the argument applies for a general solution
w
. We refer to
[
45
]
for a similar idea in the context of the modified Benjamin-Ono equation.

Proof.

Estimate (
4.33
) follows directly from (
4.31
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

We use (
3.5
) and (
2.26
) to compute, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{K}_{i}

\displaystyle=\int\partial_{t}\epsilon K_{i}+\int\epsilon\partial_{t}K_{i}

\displaystyle=k_{1}^{i}+k_{2}^{i}+k_{3}^{i}+k_{4}^{i}+k_{5}^{i}+k_{6}^{i}+k_{7}^{i}+k_{8}^{i}+k_{9}^{i}+k_{10}^{i},

where

\displaystyle k_{1}^{i}

\displaystyle:=\int\partial_{x}\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-2R_{i}\epsilon\right)K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{2}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-\int\partial_{x}\left(2(R_{1}+R_{2}+V_{A}-R_{i})\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{3}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-(-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1})\int\partial_{x}R_{1}K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{4}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-(-\dot{z}_{2}+\mu_{2}+\alpha_{2})\int\partial_{x}R_{2}K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{5}^{i}

\displaystyle:=\dot{\omega}_{1}\int\partial_{y}R_{1}K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{6}^{i}

\displaystyle:=\dot{\omega}_{2}\int\partial_{y}R_{2}K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{7}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-(\dot{\mu}_{1}+\gamma_{1})\int\Lambda R_{1}K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{8}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-(\dot{\mu}_{2}+\gamma_{2})\int\Lambda R_{2}K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{9}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-\int(\partial_{x}S+T)K_{i},

\displaystyle k_{10}^{i}

\displaystyle:=\int\epsilon\partial_{t}K_{i}.

Estimate for
k_{1}^{i}
. We claim that, for
i=1,2
,

|k_{1}^{i}|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\mu_{i}|\right).

(4.35)

By integrating by parts and recalling the definition of
L_{R_{i}}
in (
2.17
), we have

\displaystyle k_{1}^{1}

\displaystyle=-\int(L_{R_{1}}\epsilon)\partial_{y}R_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}+\mu_{1}\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{i}\chi_{\mu_{0}}-\int\left(-\Delta\epsilon+\epsilon-2R_{1}\epsilon\right)\widetilde{K}_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=k_{1,1}^{1}+k_{1,2}^{1}+k_{1,3}^{1}.

More integration by parts, (
4.30
) and the identity
L_{R_{1}}\partial_{y}R_{1}=0
yield

\displaystyle|k_{1,1}^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int\epsilon\partial^{2}_{xy}R_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\right|+\left|\int\epsilon\partial_{y}R_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime\prime}\right|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\mu_{0};

\displaystyle|k_{1,2}^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}|\mu_{1}|;

\displaystyle|k_{1,3}^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\|\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}},

which concludes the proof of (
4.35
) in the case
i=1
. The proof in the case
i=2
is similar.

Estimate for
k_{2}^{i}
. We claim that, for
i=1,2
,

|k_{2}^{i}|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\right).

(4.36)

Indeed, we observe integrating by parts and using Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle|k_{2}^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int\left(2\left(R_{2}+V_{A}\right)\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\partial_{y}R_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}\right|+\left|\int\left(2\left(R_{2}+V_{A}\right)\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\widetilde{K}_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|V_{A}\|_{L^{2}}+\|R_{1}\partial_{y}R_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\chi^{\prime}_{\mu_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\right),

which combined to (
2.36
), (
C.7
) and (
4.30
) yields (
4.36
) in the case
i=1
. The proof in the case
i=2
is similar.

Estimate for
k_{3}^{i}
. By using Lemma
B.1
,
k_{3}^{1}=0
. We claim that

|k_{3}^{2}|\lesssim\left|-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}\right|\left(\mu_{0}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\right).

(4.37)

By integrating by parts, using the orthogonality relation
\langle Q,\partial_{y}Q\rangle=0
(see (
B.3
)), and the pointwise bound
Q(\mathbf{x})\lesssim e^{-|\mathbf{x}|}
, we have

\displaystyle|k_{3}^{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}\right|\left(\left|\int R_{1}\partial_{y}R_{1}(1-\chi_{\mu_{0}})\right|+\left|\int R_{1}\widetilde{K}_{1}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}\right|\left(e^{-\mu_{0}^{-1}}+\mu_{0}\right),

since, on the time interval
[-T_{1},T_{1}]
,

|z_{1}(t)|\leq 2|Z(T_{1})|=2\rho^{-1}Z_{0}\leq 8\rho^{-1}\left|\ln(\mu_{0})\right|\leq\frac{1}{8\mu_{0}}.

(4.38)

On the other hand, we have, after integrating by parts,

\displaystyle|k_{3}^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}\right|\left(\left|\int R_{1}\partial_{y}R_{2}\chi_{\mu_{0}}\right|+\left|\int R_{1}\widetilde{K}_{2}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|-\dot{z}_{1}+\mu_{1}+\alpha_{1}\right|\left(\|R_{1}\partial_{y}R_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\mu_{0}\right),

Combining these estimates to (
C.7
) implies (
4.37
).

Estimate for
k_{4}^{i}
. By using Lemma
B.1
,
k_{4}^{2}=0
. By arguing exactly as for (
4.37
), we have

|k_{4}^{1}|\lesssim\left|-\dot{z}_{2}+\mu_{2}+\alpha_{2}\right|\left(\mu_{0}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}\right).

(4.39)

Estimate for
k_{5}^{1}
. We claim that

\left|k_{5}^{1}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|\lesssim\left|\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|\left(e^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}}+|\mu_{1}|\right),

(4.40)

where
c_{Q}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{y}\left(\int_{x}\partial_{y}Q(x,y)dx\right)^{2}dy>0
.

Indeed,

\displaystyle k_{5}^{1}=-\dot{\omega}_{1}\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\tilde{K}_{1}+\dot{\omega}_{1}\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\tilde{K}_{1}(1-\chi_{\mu_{0}}).

On the one hand, by using (
4.38
), we bound the second term on the right-hand side of the above inequality by
\left|\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|e^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}}
. On the other hand, a direct computation provides that

\left|\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\tilde{K}_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{y}\left(\int_{x}\partial_{y}Q(x,y)dx\right)^{2}dy\right|\lesssim|\mu_{1}|,

which implies (
4.40
).

Estimate for
k_{5}^{2}
. We claim that

\left|k_{5}^{2}-2c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|\lesssim\left|\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|\left(e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|\mu_{i}|+|\omega_{i}|\right)\right).

(4.41)

Indeed, we decompose
k_{5}^{2}
as

\displaystyle k_{5}^{2}

\displaystyle=\dot{\omega}_{1}\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\partial_{y}R_{2}(\tilde{x},y)d\tilde{x}\right)-\dot{\omega}_{1}\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\left(\int_{x}^{+\infty}\partial_{y}R_{2}(\tilde{x},y)d\tilde{x}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\int\partial_{y}R_{1}(x,y)\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x}\partial_{y}R_{2}(\tilde{x},y)d\tilde{x}\right)\left(1-\chi_{\mu_{0}}\right)

By arguing as in the proofs of Lemmata
C.4
and
C.5
, we have

\left|\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\left(\int_{x}^{+\infty}\partial_{y}R_{2}d\tilde{x}\right)\right|\lesssim e^{-\frac{15}{16}z}.

Moreover, we infer, by using the pointwise bound
|\partial_{y}Q(\mathbf{x})|\lesssim e^{-|\mathbf{x}|}

\left|\int\partial_{y}R_{1}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x}\partial_{y}R_{2}d\tilde{x}\right)\left(1-\chi_{\mu_{0}}\right)\right|\lesssim e^{-\mu_{0}^{-1}}.

Finally, observe with Lemma
B.4
that

\left|\int\partial_{y}R_{1}(x,y)\left(\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\partial_{y}R_{2}(\tilde{x},y)d\tilde{x}\right)dxdy-\int_{y}\left(\int_{x}\partial_{y}Q(x,y)dx\right)^{2}dy\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|\mu_{i}|+|\omega_{i}|\right).

Gathering the previous estimates yields (
4.41
).

Estimate for
k_{6}^{1}
. We have that

|k_{6}^{1}|\leq\left|\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\left|\int\partial_{y}R_{2}K_{1}\right|\lesssim\left|\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|e^{-\frac{15}{16}z},

(4.42)

where the last estimate is obtained arguing as in the proofs of Lemmata
C.4
and
C.5
.

Estimate for
k_{6}^{2}
. Arguing exactly as for
k_{5}^{1}
, we infer that

\left|k_{6}^{2}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\lesssim\left|\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\left(e^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}}+|\mu_{2}|\right).

(4.43)

Estimate for
k_{7}^{i}
and
k_{8}^{i}
. It follows directly from the fact that
\Lambda Q
is an integrable function that, for
i=1,2
,

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|k_{7}^{i}|+|k_{8}^{i}|\right)\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\dot{\mu}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\right|.

(4.44)

Estimate for
k_{9}^{i}
. We claim that, for
i=1,2
,

|k_{9}^{i}|\lesssim\left|\int S\partial_{y}R_{i}\right|+\|S\|_{L^{2}}\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.45)

Indeed, we find after integrating by parts,

\displaystyle|k_{9}^{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int S\partial_{y}R_{i}\right|+\left|\int S\partial_{y}R_{i}(1-\chi_{\mu_{0}})\right|+\left|\int S\widetilde{K}_{i}\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\right|+\left|\int T\widetilde{K}_{i}\chi_{\mu_{0}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int S\partial_{y}R_{i}\right|+\|S\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|\chi_{\mu_{0}}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+e^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}}\right)+\|T\|_{L^{2}}\|\chi_{\mu_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},

which combined to (
4.30
) yields (
4.45
).

Estimate for
k_{10}^{i}
. We claim that

|k_{10}^{i}|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{z}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.46)

Indeed, by using
\partial_{t}\partial_{y}R_{i}=\dot{\mu_{1}}\partial_{y}\Lambda R_{i}-\dot{z}_{1}\partial^{2}_{xy}R_{i}-\dot{\omega}_{1}\partial^{2}_{yy}R_{i}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that

|k_{10}^{i}|\lesssim\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(|\dot{\mu}_{i}|+|\dot{z}_{i}|+|\dot{\omega}_{i}|\right)\|{\bf 1}_{(-\mu_{0}^{-1},+\infty)}\chi_{\mu_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},

which implies (
4.46
) in view of (
4.30
).

Therefore, we conclude the proof of (
4.34
) by combining the previous estimates to the rough bounds (
4.26
).
∎

Gathering (
4.34
), Proposition
3.3
and (
4.27
)-(
4.28
) , we obtain the simplified bound

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{K}_{1}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|+\left|\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{K}_{2}-2\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}\right)+\|\epsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(4.47)

4.4.
Bootstrap of (
4.16
)-(
4.18
) on
[-T_{1},-T_{2}]
.

In this subsection, we prove that
T^{\star}>-T_{2}
by improving (
4.16
)-(
4.18
) on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
. In every step of the proof below, we will take
\mu_{0}>0
small enough possibly depending on
\eta
.

Closing the bound on
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}
on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
.
From its definition in (
3.32
), the following bound on
\Theta
holds, with (
2.43
), Proposition
3.3
, (
4.27
)-(
4.28
) and (
4.16
),

\displaystyle\Theta(t)\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{11}{4}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}.

(4.48)

By the estimate of the functional
\mathcal{F}_{-}
in Proposition
3.10
, we have, from (
4.3
), (
4.16
), (
4.27
)-(
4.28
), (
4.48
), (
4.26
) and by choosing
\mu_{0}
small enough,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{-}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{11}{4}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{9}{2}}e^{-\rho Z}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{21}{4}}.

(4.49)

On the time interval
[-T_{1},-T_{2}]
, with (
4.15
), we have
-\dot{Z}(t)\gtrsim\eta\mu_{0}
. Thus by multiplying the right-hand side of the previous inequality by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}
, an integration from
-T_{1}
to
t
provides with (
4.4
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{-}(t)-\mathcal{F}_{-}(-T_{1})\lesssim\eta^{-1}\left(\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}e^{-\rho Z(t)}+Z_{0}\mu_{0}^{\frac{17}{4}}\right)\lesssim Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}},

(4.50)

by using (
4.3
). Thus, using (
3.33
), (
4.27
) and (
4.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\forall t\in[-T_{1},T^{\star}),\quad\|\epsilon(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}.

(4.51)

Closing the bounds on
\bar{\mu}
,
\bar{z}
on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
. From (
4.24
)-(
4.25
) and (
4.51
), we deduce

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\bar{\mu}}\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\dot{\bar{z}}-\bar{\mu}\right|\lesssim Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}.

After multiplying the first inequality by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}
, an integration from
-T_{1}
to
t
provides, with (
4.10
),

\displaystyle\left|\bar{\mu}(t)\right|\lesssim\left|\bar{\mu}(-T_{1})\right|+\eta^{-1}\left(e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}+Z_{0}^{-5}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}\right)\lesssim Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}.

(4.52)

Arguing similarly for
\bar{z}
, one obtains

\displaystyle\left|\bar{z}(t)\right|\lesssim\left|\bar{z}(-T_{1})\right|+\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}\lesssim Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(4.53)

Closing the bounds on
\omega
and
\bar{\omega}
on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
. From (
4.47
), with
c_{Q}\neq 0
, it holds

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{K}_{1}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|+\left|\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{K}_{2}-2\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\lesssim Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{9}{4}}.

After multiplying by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}
, an integration from
-T_{1}
to
t
gives, with (
4.33
), (
4.51
) and (
4.11
)

\displaystyle\left|\omega_{1}(t)-\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\omega_{2}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\omega_{1}(-T_{1})-\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\omega_{2}(-T_{1})+\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|+Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}+\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}.

(4.54)

Closing the bounds on
\mu
and
z
on
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
with
T^{\star}\leq-T_{3}
. We recall that
T_{3}
is defined in (
4.14
). We infer from (
4.24
)-(
4.25
) and (
4.51
) that

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\mu}+2\gamma_{1}(z)\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\dot{z}-\mu\right|\lesssim Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}.

(4.55)

From (
2.40
) and (
D.8
) we infer

\displaystyle\left|\gamma_{1}(z(t))-\gamma_{1}(Z(t))\right|\lesssim|z(t)-Z(t)|\sup_{|Y-Z(t)|\leq|Z(t)-z(t)|}\left|\gamma_{1}^{\prime}(Y)\right|\lesssim|z(t)-Z(t)|\frac{e^{-Z(t)}}{Z(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(4.56)

Moreover,
\ddot{Z}=-2\gamma_{1}(Z)
(see (
2.40
) and (
4.1
)). Thus, it follows from (
4.17
)

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\mu}-\ddot{Z}\right|\lesssim\left|z-Z\right|Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}\lesssim Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}

By multiplying by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}
, an integration provides

\displaystyle\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\mu(-T_{1})-\dot{Z}(-T_{1})\right|+\eta^{-1}\left(Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}+Z_{0}^{-5}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}},

(4.57)

where the last bounds follows by using that
Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{Z(t)}\leq Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{2}
on
[-T_{1},-T_{3}]
(see the definition of
T_{3}
in (
4.14
)) and from (
4.10
). Then, we deduce gathering the above estimate, (
4.10
) and (
4.55
) that

\displaystyle\left|\dot{z}-\dot{Z}\right|\leq\left|\dot{z}-\mu\right|+\left|\mu-\dot{Z}\right|\lesssim Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}+\eta^{-1}\left(Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}+e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}+Z_{0}^{-5}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}\right),

which, after a multiplication by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}
, implies with (
4.11
) and the definition of
T_{3}
in (
4.14
)

\displaystyle\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|z(-T_{1})-Z(-T_{1})\right|+\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta^{-2}\left(Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-\frac{5}{4}}Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}+\mu_{0}^{-1}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}+Z_{0}^{-4}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\lesssim\eta^{-2}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(4.58)

By using the bounds (
4.51
), (
4.52
), (
4.53
), (
4.54
), (
4.57
) and (
4.58
), we strictly improve the bounds (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) on the time interval
[-T_{1},T^{\star})
with
T^{\star}\leq-T_{3}
. This implies that
T^{\star}>-T_{3}
.

Closing the bounds on
\mu
and
z
on
[-T_{3},T^{\star})
. In order to study the dynamical parameters
\mu
and
z
, we introduce the approximate Hamiltonian
H
defined by

\displaystyle H(t):=\frac{1}{2}\mu(t)^{2}+\frac{2}{\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q(x+z(t),y)Q^{2}(x,y)dxdy.

(4.59)

We claim that

\displaystyle\left|H(-T_{3})-2\mu_{0}^{2}\right|\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z(-T_{3})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(-T_{3})}.

(4.60)

Indeed, (
4.57
) at time
-T_{3}
provides

\displaystyle\left|\mu(-T_{3})^{2}-\dot{Z}(-T_{3})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\mu(-T_{3})-\dot{Z}(-T_{3})+2\dot{Z}(-T_{3})\right)\left(\mu(-T_{3})-\dot{Z}(-T_{3})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{11}{4}}.

(4.61)

With (
D.8
) and (
4.11
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(Q(\cdot+z(-T_{3}),\cdot)-Q(\cdot+Z(-T_{3}),\cdot)\right)Q^{2}\right|\lesssim Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z(-T_{3})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(-T_{3})}.

Gathering those computations with (
4.3
), the definition of
Z
in Lemma
4.1
and the fact that
H(Z(t),\dot{Z(t)})=2\mu_{0}^{2}
(see Lemma
D.2
) yield (
4.60
).

On
[-T_{3};T^{\star})
, we compute the time derivative of
H
by using (
2.39
) and (
2.40
),

\displaystyle\left|\dot{H}(t)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\dot{\mu}(t)\mu(t)+\dot{z}(t)2\gamma_{1}(z(t))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\dot{\mu}(t)+2\gamma_{1}(z(t))\right|\left|\mu(t)\right|+2\left|\gamma_{1}(z(t))\right|\left|\dot{z}(t)-\mu(t)\right|.

(4.62)

From (
4.24
)-(
4.26
), (
4.51
) and (
4.16
), it follows

\displaystyle\left|\dot{H}\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}^{2}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}\lesssim Z_{0}^{-3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z},

(4.63)

where we used in the last inequality that
\mu_{0}^{2}\leq Z_{0}Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}
for
t\in[-T_{3},-T_{2}]
.
We multiply the right-hand side of (
4.63
) by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}
(recall (
4.15
)), integrate from
-T_{3}
to
t
and use (
4.60
) to get

\displaystyle\left|H(t)-2\mu_{0}^{2}\right|\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}.

From (
4.20
) and (
D.8
), we have

\displaystyle Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}\lesssim\frac{2}{\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q(x+z(t),y)Q^{2}(x,y)dxdy.

Moreover, we infer from (
4.24
), (
4.51
) and recalling
|\dot{Z}(t)|\leq 2\mu_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mu(t)^{2}-\dot{z}(t)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mu(t)-\dot{z}(t)\right|\left(\left|\dot{z}(t)-\mu(t)\right|+2\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|+2|\dot{Z}(t)|\right)\lesssim Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)e^{-Z(t)}.

It follows from the three previous inequalities and (
4.20
) that, by defining
\nu:=C\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}
for a constant
C
large enough,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\dot{z}(t)^{2}+\frac{2(1-\nu)}{\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q(x+z(t),y)Q^{2}(x,y)dxdy

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\mu_{0}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\dot{z}(t)^{2}+\frac{2(1+\nu)}{\langle\Lambda Q,Q\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q(x+z(t),y)Q^{2}(x,y)dxdy.

We can thus apply Proposition
D.14
with
h=2\mu_{0}^{2}
and
\varepsilon_{0}=Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}
(see (
4.17
)) to obtain that for any
t\in[-T_{3},T^{\star})
,

\displaystyle\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|\lesssim\left(\nu+\varepsilon_{0}\right)\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(4.64)

To close the bound on
\mu
, we use (
4.25
), with (
4.64
) in (
4.56
), (
4.51
) and
\mu_{0}
small so that
\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}\leq 1
in (
4.51
), to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\mu}-\ddot{Z}\right|=\left|\dot{\mu}+2\gamma_{1}(Z)\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+Z_{0}^{-6}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}+\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}.

After multiplying the right-hand side by
-\dot{Z}(t)\eta^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-1}\gtrsim 1
, an integration from
-T_{3}
to
t
provides, with the initial condition (
4.17
),

\displaystyle\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|\lesssim\left|\mu(-T_{3})-\dot{Z}(-T_{3})\right|+\eta^{-2}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}\lesssim\eta^{-2}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}.

(4.65)

By (
4.51
), (
4.52
), (
4.53
), (
4.54
), (
4.64
) and (
4.65
), we strictly improve (
4.16
) by taking
Z_{0}
large enough depending on
\eta
, and thus by continuity
T^{\star}>-T_{2}
.

4.5.
Bootstrap of (
4.16
) and (
4.18
) on
[-T_{2},T_{2}]
.

Assuming
T^{\star}<T_{2}
, we argue on
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
in order to prove by contradiction that
T^{\star}>T_{2}
. On this time interval, the inequality
-\dot{Z}(t)\geq c\eta\mu_{0}
does not hold anymore. We bypass this issue by using the following inequalities for
t\in[-T_{2},T_{2}]
, deduced from (
4.3
), (
4.13
), (
D.8
) and (
D.1
),

\displaystyle\mu_{0}^{2}\lesssim Z(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z(t)}\lesssim\ddot{Z}(t).

(4.66)

Closing the bounds on
\mu
and
\bar{\mu}
on
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
. From (
4.25
), (
4.56
), (
4.16
), (
4.18
), (
4.66
), we have for
t\in[-T_{2},T^{\star})
,

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\mu}+2\gamma_{1}(Z)\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{\mu}}\right|\lesssim\left|z-Z\right|Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}\ddot{Z}.

From (
4.52
) and (
4.65
), an integration from
-T_{2}
to
t
provides, with the upper bound
\left|\dot{Z}(-T_{2})\right|\lesssim\eta\mu_{0}
(see Lemma
D.9
),

\displaystyle\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|+\left|\bar{\mu}(t)\right|\lesssim\eta^{-2}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}\left|\dot{Z}(-T_{2})\right|\lesssim\left(\eta^{-2}Z_{0}^{-1}+\eta\right)\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}.

(4.67)

Closing the bounds on
z
and
\bar{z}
on
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
. From (
4.24
), (
4.67
), (
4.66
) and (
4.16
), we have for
t\in[-T_{2},T^{\star})
,

\displaystyle\left|\dot{z}(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|+\left|\dot{\bar{z}}(t)\right|\lesssim Z^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-Z}+\mu_{0}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\ddot{Z}(t),

and an integration from
-T_{2}
to
t
provides with (
4.64
) and (
4.53
) at time
-T_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|z(t)-Z(t)\right|+\left|\bar{z}(t)\right|\lesssim\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}+\mu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\dot{Z}(-T_{2})|\lesssim\left(\eta^{-1}Z_{0}^{-1}+\eta\right)\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(4.68)

Closing the bounds on
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}
on
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
.
On
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
, since
\left|\dot{\mu}(t)-\ddot{Z}(t)\right|\leq C\mu_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}\ddot{Z}(t)
, by taking
\mu_{0}
small we have
\dot{\mu}(t)>c\mu_{0}^{2}
for some small constant
c
and
\mu
is an increasing function. Since
-\dot{Z}(-T_{2})=\dot{Z}(T_{2})\geq c\eta\mu_{0}
and
\left|\mu(t)-\dot{Z}(t)\right|\leq C\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{4}}
, we have that
\mu(-T_{2})\leq-c\eta\mu_{0}
and
\mu(T_{2})\geq c\eta\mu_{0}
. Hence, we deduce from the intermediate value theorem, assuming
T^{\star}
large enough, that there exists a unique time
t_{0}
, such that
\mu(t_{0})=0
. In particular, it implies
\mu(t)<0
for
t<t_{0}
and
\mu(t)>0
for
t>t_{0}
.

To control
\epsilon
on
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
, we use the functional
\mathcal{F}_{-}
on the time interval
[-T_{2},t_{0}]
and the functional
\mathcal{F}_{+}
on the interval
[t_{0},T^{\star})
. Arguing as in (
4.49
) and using that
0<\rho<\frac{1}{32}
, we obtain the following bound

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{-}(t)\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{11}{4}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{9}{2}}e^{-\rho Z}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{21}{4}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}+\rho}\ddot{Z}.

If
T^{\star}>t_{0}
, the same bound holds for
\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{+}
on
[t_{0},T^{\star})
. In both cases, an integration from
-T_{2}
to
T^{\star}
, the fact that
\left|\dot{Z}(-T_{2})\right|\lesssim\eta\mu_{0}
and the estimate at
-T_{2}
in (
4.50
) give the following bound on
\epsilon
for
t\in[-T_{2},T^{\star})

\displaystyle\|\epsilon(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\eta Z_{0}^{-4}\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}.

(4.69)

Closing the bounds on
\omega
and
\bar{\omega}
on
[-T_{2},T^{\star})
. From (
4.47
), with
c_{1}\neq 0
, it holds with (
4.66
)

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{K}_{1}-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{1}\right|+\left|\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{K}_{2}-2\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)-c_{Q}\dot{\omega}_{2}\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{9}{4}}\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\ddot{Z}.

(4.70)

By using
\left|\dot{Z}(-T_{2})\right|\lesssim\eta\mu_{0}
, an integration from
-T_{2}
to
t
gives, with (
4.33
), (
4.69
) and (
4.54
)

\displaystyle\left|\omega_{1}(t)-\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\omega_{2}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\omega_{1}(-T_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|+\left|\omega_{2}(-T_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\omega_{0}\right|+\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}Z_{0}^{-2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}+\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\dot{Z}(-T_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\mu_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}.

(4.71)

We now choose
\eta
small enough so that (
4.67
), (
4.68
), (
4.69
) and (
4.71
) strictly improves (
4.16
) and (
4.18
) on
[-T_{2};T^{\star})
, and thus we conclude that
T^{\star}>T_{2}
.

4.6.
Bootstrap of (
4.19
) on
[T_{2},T_{1}]

We now prove that
T^{\star}=T_{1}
by improving (
4.19
) on
[T_{2},T^{\star})
. We recall that the constant
\eta
is fixed. In every step of the proof below, we will take
\mu_{0}>0
small enough possibly depending on
C_{1}
.

Closing the bound on
\|\epsilon\|_{H^{1}}
on
[T_{2},T^{\star})
. From (
3.32
), it holds, with (
2.43
), (
4.19
), (
4.24
)-(
4.25
) and (
4.27
)-(
4.28
),

\displaystyle\Theta(t)\lesssim C_{1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{11}{4}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}.

(4.72)

By using the function
\mathcal{F}_{+}
defined in (
3.25
), we get, with (
3.34
), (
4.26
), (
4.19
), (
4.27
)-(
4.28
) and (
4.72
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{+}(t)\lesssim C_{1}\mu_{0}^{\frac{11}{4}}e^{-\frac{15}{16}Z(t)}+C_{1}^{2}\mu_{0}^{\frac{9}{2}}e^{-\rho Z(t)}+C_{1}^{3}\mu_{0}^{\frac{21}{4}}.

Using that
\dot{Z}
is an increasing function, (
4.15
) implies that
\dot{Z}(t)\gtrsim\mu_{0}
for
t\geq T_{2}
(recall that
\eta
is fixed). By multiplying the previous term by
\dot{Z}(t)\mu_{0}^{-1}\gtrsim 1
and integrating over
[T_{2},t)
with
\left|\mathcal{F}_{+}(T_{2})\right|\lesssim\mu_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}
(see (
4.16
)), we obtain for any
t\in[T_{2},T^{\star})
,
